src/requests/adapters.py:158:class HTTPAdapter(BaseAdapter):
src/requests/adapters.py:159:    """The built-in HTTP Adapter for urllib3.
src/requests/adapters.py:160:
src/requests/adapters.py:161:    Provides a general-case interface for Requests sessions to contact HTTP and
src/requests/adapters.py:162:    HTTPS urls by implementing the Transport Adapter interface. This class will
src/requests/adapters.py:163:    usually be created by the :class:`Session <Session>` class under the
src/requests/adapters.py:164:    covers.
src/requests/adapters.py:165:
src/requests/adapters.py:166:    :param pool_connections: The number of urllib3 connection pools to cache.
src/requests/adapters.py:167:    :param pool_maxsize: The maximum number of connections to save in the pool.
src/requests/adapters.py:168:    :param max_retries: The maximum number of retries each connection
src/requests/adapters.py:169:        should attempt. Note, this applies only to failed DNS lookups, socket
src/requests/adapters.py:170:        connections and connection timeouts, never to requests where data has
src/requests/adapters.py:171:        made it to the server. By default, Requests does not retry failed
src/requests/adapters.py:172:        connections. If you need granular control over the conditions under
src/requests/adapters.py:173:        which we retry a request, import urllib3's ``Retry`` class and pass
src/requests/adapters.py:174:        that instead.
src/requests/adapters.py:175:    :param pool_block: Whether the connection pool should block for connections.
src/requests/adapters.py:176:
src/requests/adapters.py:177:    Usage::
src/requests/adapters.py:178:
src/requests/adapters.py:179:      >>> import requests
src/requests/adapters.py:180:      >>> s = requests.Session()
src/requests/adapters.py:181:      >>> a = requests.adapters.HTTPAdapter(max_retries=3)
src/requests/adapters.py:182:      >>> s.mount('http://', a)
src/requests/adapters.py:183:    """

src/requests/adapters.py:403:    def build_connection_pool_key_attributes(
src/requests/adapters.py:404:        self, request: PreparedRequest, verify: _t.VerifyType, cert: _t.CertType = None
src/requests/adapters.py:405:    ) -> tuple[dict[str, Any], dict[str, Any]]:
src/requests/adapters.py:406:        """Build the PoolKey attributes used by urllib3 to return a connection.
src/requests/adapters.py:407:
src/requests/adapters.py:408:        This looks at the PreparedRequest, the user-specified verify value,
src/requests/adapters.py:409:        and the value of the cert parameter to determine what PoolKey values
src/requests/adapters.py:410:        to use to select a connection from a given urllib3 Connection Pool.
src/requests/adapters.py:411:
src/requests/adapters.py:412:        The SSL related pool key arguments are not consistently set. As of
src/requests/adapters.py:413:        this writing, use the following to determine what keys may be in that
src/requests/adapters.py:414:        dictionary:
src/requests/adapters.py:415:
src/requests/adapters.py:416:        * If ``verify`` is ``True``, ``"ssl_context"`` will be set and will be the
src/requests/adapters.py:417:          default Requests SSL Context
src/requests/adapters.py:418:        * If ``verify`` is ``False``, ``"ssl_context"`` will not be set but
src/requests/adapters.py:419:          ``"cert_reqs"`` will be set
src/requests/adapters.py:420:        * If ``verify`` is a string, (i.e., it is a user-specified trust bundle)
src/requests/adapters.py:421:          ``"ca_certs"`` will be set if the string is not a directory recognized
src/requests/adapters.py:422:          by :py:func:`os.path.isdir`, otherwise ``"ca_cert_dir"`` will be
src/requests/adapters.py:423:          set.
src/requests/adapters.py:424:        * If ``"cert"`` is specified, ``"cert_file"`` will always be set. If
src/requests/adapters.py:425:          ``"cert"`` is a tuple with a second item, ``"key_file"`` will also
src/requests/adapters.py:426:          be present
src/requests/adapters.py:427:
src/requests/adapters.py:428:        To override these settings, one may subclass this class, call this
src/requests/adapters.py:429:        method and use the above logic to change parameters as desired. For
src/requests/adapters.py:430:        example, if one wishes to use a custom :py:class:`ssl.SSLContext` one
src/requests/adapters.py:431:        must both set ``"ssl_context"`` and based on what else they require,
src/requests/adapters.py:432:        alter the other keys to ensure the desired behaviour.
src/requests/adapters.py:433:
src/requests/adapters.py:434:        :param request:
src/requests/adapters.py:435:            The PreparedRequest being sent over the connection.
src/requests/adapters.py:436:        :type request:
src/requests/adapters.py:437:            :class:`~requests.models.PreparedRequest`
src/requests/adapters.py:438:        :param verify:
src/requests/adapters.py:439:            Either a boolean, in which case it controls whether
src/requests/adapters.py:440:            we verify the server's TLS certificate, or a string, in which case it
src/requests/adapters.py:441:            must be a path to a CA bundle to use.
src/requests/adapters.py:442:        :param cert:
src/requests/adapters.py:443:            (optional) Any user-provided SSL certificate for client
src/requests/adapters.py:444:            authentication (a.k.a., mTLS). This may be a string (i.e., just
src/requests/adapters.py:445:            the path to a file which holds both certificate and key) or a
src/requests/adapters.py:446:            tuple of length 2 with the certificate file path and key file
src/requests/adapters.py:447:            path.
src/requests/adapters.py:448:        :returns:
src/requests/adapters.py:449:            A tuple of two dictionaries. The first is the "host parameters"
src/requests/adapters.py:450:            portion of the Pool Key including scheme, hostname, and port. The
src/requests/adapters.py:451:            second is a dictionary of SSLContext related parameters.
src/requests/adapters.py:452:        """
src/requests/adapters.py:453:        return _urllib3_request_context(request, verify, cert, self.poolmanager)
src/requests/adapters.py:454:

src/requests/help.py:43:    doesn't work for Jython or IronPython. Future investigation should be done
src/requests/help.py:44:    to work out the correct shape of the code for those platforms.
src/requests/help.py:45:    """

src/requests/help.py:47:
src/requests/help.py:48:    if implementation == "CPython":
src/requests/help.py:49:        implementation_version = platform.python_version()
src/requests/help.py:50:    elif implementation == "PyPy":
src/requests/help.py:51:        pypy = sys.pypy_version_info  # type: ignore[attr-defined]
src/requests/help.py:52:        implementation_version = f"{pypy.major}.{pypy.minor}.{pypy.micro}"  # pyright: ignore[reportUnknownMemberType]
src/requests/help.py:53:        if sys.pypy_version_info.releaselevel != "final":  # type: ignore[attr-defined]

src/requests/help.py:56:            )
src/requests/help.py:57:    elif implementation == "Jython":
src/requests/help.py:58:        implementation_version = platform.python_version()  # Complete Guess
src/requests/help.py:59:    elif implementation == "IronPython":
src/requests/help.py:60:        implementation_version = platform.python_version()  # Complete Guess
src/requests/help.py:61:    else:
src/requests/help.py:62:        implementation_version = "Unknown"
src/requests/help.py:63:
src/requests/help.py:64:    return {"name": implementation, "version": implementation_version}

src/requests/models.py:483:    def prepare_url(
src/requests/models.py:484:        self,
src/requests/models.py:485:        url: _t.UriType,
src/requests/models.py:486:        params: _t.ParamsType,
src/requests/models.py:487:    ) -> None:
src/requests/models.py:488:        """Prepares the given HTTP URL."""
src/requests/models.py:489:        #: Accept objects that have string representations.
src/requests/models.py:490:        #: We're unable to blindly call unicode/str functions
src/requests/models.py:491:        #: as this will include the bytestring indicator (b'')
src/requests/models.py:492:        #: on python 3.x.
src/requests/models.py:493:        #: https://github.com/psf/requests/pull/2238
src/requests/models.py:494:        if isinstance(url, bytes):
src/requests/models.py:495:            url = url.decode("utf8")
src/requests/models.py:496:        else:
src/requests/models.py:497:            url = str(url)
src/requests/models.py:498:
src/requests/models.py:499:        # Remove leading whitespaces from url
src/requests/models.py:500:        url = url.lstrip()
src/requests/models.py:501:
src/requests/models.py:502:        # Don't do any URL preparation for non-HTTP schemes like `mailto`,
src/requests/models.py:503:        # `data` etc to work around exceptions from `url_parse`, which
src/requests/models.py:504:        # handles RFC 3986 only.
src/requests/models.py:505:        if ":" in url and not url.lower().startswith("http"):
src/requests/models.py:506:            self.url = url
src/requests/models.py:507:            return
src/requests/models.py:508:
src/requests/models.py:509:        # Support for unicode domain names and paths.
src/requests/models.py:510:        try:
src/requests/models.py:511:            scheme, auth, host, port, path, query, fragment = parse_url(url)
src/requests/models.py:512:        except LocationParseError as e:
src/requests/models.py:513:            raise InvalidURL(*e.args)
src/requests/models.py:514:
src/requests/models.py:515:        if not scheme:
src/requests/models.py:516:            raise MissingSchema(
src/requests/models.py:517:                f"Invalid URL {url!r}: No scheme supplied. "
src/requests/models.py:518:                f"Perhaps you meant https://{url}?"
src/requests/models.py:519:            )
src/requests/models.py:520:
src/requests/models.py:521:        if not host:
src/requests/models.py:522:            raise InvalidURL(f"Invalid URL {url!r}: No host supplied")
src/requests/models.py:523:
src/requests/models.py:524:        # In general, we want to try IDNA encoding the hostname if the string contains
src/requests/models.py:525:        # non-ASCII characters. This allows users to automatically get the correct IDNA
src/requests/models.py:526:        # behaviour. For strings containing only ASCII characters, we need to also verify
src/requests/models.py:527:        # it doesn't start with a wildcard (*), before allowing the unencoded hostname.
src/requests/models.py:528:        if not unicode_is_ascii(host):
src/requests/models.py:529:            try:
src/requests/models.py:530:                host = self._get_idna_encoded_host(host)
src/requests/models.py:531:            except UnicodeError:
src/requests/models.py:532:                raise InvalidURL("URL has an invalid label.")
src/requests/models.py:533:        elif host.startswith(("*", ".")):
src/requests/models.py:534:            raise InvalidURL("URL has an invalid label.")
src/requests/models.py:535:
src/requests/models.py:536:        # Carefully reconstruct the network location
src/requests/models.py:537:        netloc = auth or ""
src/requests/models.py:538:        if netloc:
src/requests/models.py:539:            netloc += "@"
src/requests/models.py:540:        netloc += host
src/requests/models.py:541:        if port:

src/requests/models.py:543:
src/requests/models.py:544:        # Bare domains aren't valid URLs.
src/requests/models.py:545:        if not path:
src/requests/models.py:546:            path = "/"
src/requests/models.py:547:
src/requests/models.py:548:        if isinstance(params, (str, bytes)):
src/requests/models.py:549:            params = to_native_string(params)
src/requests/models.py:550:
src/requests/models.py:551:        if params is not None:

src/requests/models.py:553:        else:
src/requests/models.py:554:            enc_params = ""
src/requests/models.py:555:
src/requests/models.py:556:        if enc_params:
src/requests/models.py:557:            if query:
src/requests/models.py:558:                query = f"{query}&{enc_params}"
src/requests/models.py:559:            else:
src/requests/models.py:560:                query = enc_params
src/requests/models.py:561:
src/requests/models.py:562:        url = requote_uri(urlunparse((scheme, netloc, path, "", query, fragment)))
src/requests/models.py:563:        self.url = url

src/requests/models.py:915:        self, chunk_size: int | None = 1, decode_unicode: bool = False
src/requests/models.py:916:    ) -> Iterator[str | bytes]:
src/requests/models.py:917:        """Iterates over the response data.  When stream=True is set on the
src/requests/models.py:918:        request, this avoids reading the content at once into memory for
src/requests/models.py:919:        large responses.  The chunk size is the number of bytes it should
src/requests/models.py:920:        read into memory.  This is not necessarily the length of each item
src/requests/models.py:921:        returned as decoding can take place.
src/requests/models.py:922:
src/requests/models.py:923:        chunk_size must be of type int or None. A value of None will
src/requests/models.py:924:        function differently depending on the value of `stream`.
src/requests/models.py:925:        stream=True will read data as it arrives in whatever size the
src/requests/models.py:926:        chunks are received. If stream=False, data is returned as
src/requests/models.py:927:        a single chunk.
src/requests/models.py:928:
src/requests/models.py:929:        If decode_unicode is True, content will be decoded using encoding
src/requests/models.py:930:        information from the response. If no encoding information is available,
src/requests/models.py:931:        bytes will be returned. This can be bypassed by manually setting
src/requests/models.py:932:        `encoding` on the response.
src/requests/models.py:933:        """
src/requests/models.py:934:
src/requests/models.py:935:        def generate() -> Generator[bytes, None, None]:
src/requests/models.py:936:            # Special case for urllib3.
src/requests/models.py:937:            if hasattr(self.raw, "stream"):
src/requests/models.py:938:                try:
src/requests/models.py:939:                    yield from self.raw.stream(chunk_size, decode_content=True)
src/requests/models.py:940:                except ProtocolError as e:
src/requests/models.py:941:                    raise ChunkedEncodingError(e)
src/requests/models.py:942:                except DecodeError as e:
src/requests/models.py:943:                    raise ContentDecodingError(e)
src/requests/models.py:944:                except ReadTimeoutError as e:
src/requests/models.py:945:                    raise ConnectionError(e)
src/requests/models.py:946:                except SSLError as e:
src/requests/models.py:947:                    raise RequestsSSLError(e)
src/requests/models.py:948:            else:
src/requests/models.py:949:                # Standard file-like object.
src/requests/models.py:950:                while True:
src/requests/models.py:951:                    chunk = self.raw.read(chunk_size)
src/requests/models.py:952:                    if not chunk:
src/requests/models.py:953:                        break
src/requests/models.py:954:                    yield chunk
src/requests/models.py:955:
src/requests/models.py:956:            self._content_consumed = True

src/requests/sessions.py:138:        # on each subsequent redirect response (if any).
src/requests/sessions.py:139:        # If a custom mixin is used to handle this logic, it may be advantageous
src/requests/sessions.py:140:        # to cache the redirect location onto the response object as a private
src/requests/sessions.py:141:        # attribute.
src/requests/sessions.py:142:        if resp.is_redirect:
src/requests/sessions.py:143:            location = resp.headers["location"]
src/requests/sessions.py:144:            # Currently the underlying http module on py3 decode headers
src/requests/sessions.py:145:            # in latin1, but empirical evidence suggests that latin1 is very
src/requests/sessions.py:146:            # rarely used with non-ASCII characters in HTTP headers.
src/requests/sessions.py:147:            # It is more likely to get UTF8 header rather than latin1.
src/requests/sessions.py:148:            # This causes incorrect handling of UTF8 encoded location headers.
src/requests/sessions.py:149:            # To solve this, we re-encode the location in latin1.
src/requests/sessions.py:150:            location = location.encode("latin1")
src/requests/sessions.py:151:            return to_native_string(location, "utf8")
src/requests/sessions.py:152:        return None

src/requests/sessions.py:155:        """Decide whether Authorization header should be removed when redirecting"""
src/requests/sessions.py:156:        old_parsed = urlparse(old_url)
src/requests/sessions.py:157:        new_parsed = urlparse(new_url)
src/requests/sessions.py:158:        if old_parsed.hostname != new_parsed.hostname:
src/requests/sessions.py:159:            return True

src/requests/sessions.py:162:        # breaking backwards compatibility with older versions of requests
src/requests/sessions.py:163:        # that allowed any redirects on the same host.

src/requests/sessions.py:166:            and old_parsed.port in (80, None)
src/requests/sessions.py:167:            and new_parsed.scheme == "https"
src/requests/sessions.py:168:            and new_parsed.port in (443, None)
src/requests/sessions.py:169:        ):
src/requests/sessions.py:170:            return False
src/requests/sessions.py:171:
src/requests/sessions.py:172:        # Handle default port usage corresponding to scheme.
src/requests/sessions.py:173:        changed_port = old_parsed.port != new_parsed.port
src/requests/sessions.py:174:        changed_scheme = old_parsed.scheme != new_parsed.scheme
src/requests/sessions.py:175:        default_port = (DEFAULT_PORTS.get(old_parsed.scheme, None), None)
src/requests/sessions.py:176:        if (
src/requests/sessions.py:177:            not changed_scheme
src/requests/sessions.py:178:            and old_parsed.port in default_port
src/requests/sessions.py:179:            and new_parsed.port in default_port
src/requests/sessions.py:180:        ):

src/requests/sessions.py:182:

src/requests/structures.py:20:class CaseInsensitiveDict(MutableMapping[str, _VT], Generic[_VT]):
src/requests/structures.py:21:    """A case-insensitive ``dict``-like object.
src/requests/structures.py:22:
src/requests/structures.py:23:    Implements all methods and operations of
src/requests/structures.py:24:    ``MutableMapping`` as well as dict's ``copy``. Also
src/requests/structures.py:25:    provides ``lower_items``.
src/requests/structures.py:26:
src/requests/structures.py:27:    All keys are expected to be strings. The structure remembers the
src/requests/structures.py:28:    case of the last key to be set, and ``iter(instance)``,
src/requests/structures.py:29:    ``keys()``, ``items()``, ``iterkeys()``, and ``iteritems()``
src/requests/structures.py:30:    will contain case-sensitive keys. However, querying and contains
src/requests/structures.py:31:    testing is case insensitive::
src/requests/structures.py:32:
src/requests/structures.py:33:        cid = CaseInsensitiveDict()
src/requests/structures.py:34:        cid['Accept'] = 'application/json'
src/requests/structures.py:35:        cid['aCCEPT'] == 'application/json'  # True
src/requests/structures.py:36:        list(cid) == ['Accept']  # True
src/requests/structures.py:37:
src/requests/structures.py:38:    For example, ``headers['content-encoding']`` will return the
src/requests/structures.py:39:    value of a ``'Content-Encoding'`` response header, regardless
src/requests/structures.py:40:    of how the header name was originally stored.
src/requests/structures.py:41:
src/requests/structures.py:42:    If the constructor, ``.update``, or equality comparison
src/requests/structures.py:43:    operations are given keys that have equal ``.lower()``s, the
src/requests/structures.py:44:    behavior is undefined.
src/requests/structures.py:45:    """
src/requests/structures.py:46:
src/requests/structures.py:47:    _store: OrderedDict[str, tuple[str, _VT]]
src/requests/structures.py:48:
src/requests/structures.py:49:    def __init__(
src/requests/structures.py:50:        self,
src/requests/structures.py:51:        data: Mapping[str, _VT] | Iterable[tuple[str, _VT]] | None = None,
src/requests/structures.py:52:        **kwargs: _VT,
src/requests/structures.py:53:    ) -> None:
src/requests/structures.py:54:        self._store = OrderedDict()
src/requests/structures.py:55:        if data is None:
src/requests/structures.py:56:            data = {}
src/requests/structures.py:57:        self.update(data, **kwargs)
src/requests/structures.py:58:
src/requests/structures.py:59:    def __setitem__(self, key: str, value: _VT) -> None:
src/requests/structures.py:60:        # Use the lowercased key for lookups, but store the actual
src/requests/structures.py:61:        # key alongside the value.
src/requests/structures.py:62:        self._store[key.lower()] = (key, value)
src/requests/structures.py:63:
src/requests/structures.py:64:    def __getitem__(self, key: str) -> _VT:
src/requests/structures.py:65:        return self._store[key.lower()][1]
src/requests/structures.py:66:
src/requests/structures.py:67:    def __delitem__(self, key: str) -> None:
src/requests/structures.py:68:        del self._store[key.lower()]
src/requests/structures.py:69:

src/requests/structures.py:71:        return (casedkey for casedkey, _ in self._store.values())
src/requests/structures.py:72:
src/requests/structures.py:73:    def __len__(self) -> int:
src/requests/structures.py:74:        return len(self._store)
src/requests/structures.py:75:

src/requests/structures.py:77:        """Like iteritems(), but with all lowercase keys."""
src/requests/structures.py:78:        return ((lowerkey, keyval[1]) for (lowerkey, keyval) in self._store.items())
src/requests/structures.py:79:
src/requests/structures.py:80:    def __eq__(self, other: object) -> bool:
src/requests/structures.py:81:        if isinstance(other, Mapping):
src/requests/structures.py:82:            other_dict: CaseInsensitiveDict[Any] = CaseInsensitiveDict(other)  # type: ignore[reportUnknownArgumentType]
src/requests/structures.py:83:        else:
src/requests/structures.py:84:            return NotImplemented
src/requests/structures.py:85:        # Compare insensitively

src/requests/structures.py:87:

src/requests/utils.py:295:    if os.path.exists(path):

src/requests/utils.py:297:        return path
src/requests/utils.py:298:
src/requests/utils.py:299:    # find the first valid part of the provided path and treat that as a zip archive
src/requests/utils.py:300:    # assume the rest of the path is the name of a member in the archive
src/requests/utils.py:301:    archive, member = os.path.split(path)
src/requests/utils.py:302:    while archive and not os.path.exists(archive):
src/requests/utils.py:303:        archive, prefix = os.path.split(archive)
src/requests/utils.py:304:        if not prefix:

src/requests/utils.py:306:            # we _can_ end up in an infinite loop on a rare corner case affecting a small number of users

src/requests/utils.py:315:        return path
src/requests/utils.py:316:
src/requests/utils.py:317:    # we have a valid zip archive and a valid member of that archive

src/requests/utils.py:320:    try:
src/requests/utils.py:321:        os.write(fd, zip_file.read(member))

src/requests/utils.py:409:    """Parse lists as described by RFC 2068 Section 2.
src/requests/utils.py:410:
src/requests/utils.py:411:    In particular, parse comma-separated lists where the elements of
src/requests/utils.py:412:    the list may include quoted-strings.  A quoted-string could
src/requests/utils.py:413:    contain a comma.  A non-quoted string could have quotes in the
src/requests/utils.py:414:    middle.  Quotes are removed automatically after parsing.
src/requests/utils.py:415:
src/requests/utils.py:416:    It basically works like :func:`parse_set_header` just that items
src/requests/utils.py:417:    may appear multiple times and case sensitivity is preserved.
src/requests/utils.py:418:
src/requests/utils.py:419:    The return value is a standard :class:`list`:
src/requests/utils.py:420:
src/requests/utils.py:421:    >>> parse_list_header('token, "quoted value"')
src/requests/utils.py:422:    ['token', 'quoted value']
src/requests/utils.py:423:
src/requests/utils.py:424:    To create a header from the :class:`list` again, use the
src/requests/utils.py:425:    :func:`dump_header` function.
src/requests/utils.py:426:
src/requests/utils.py:427:    :param value: a string with a list header.
src/requests/utils.py:428:    :return: :class:`list`
src/requests/utils.py:429:    :rtype: list
src/requests/utils.py:430:    """
src/requests/utils.py:431:    result: list[str] = []
src/requests/utils.py:432:    for item in _parse_list_header(value):
src/requests/utils.py:433:        if item[:1] == item[-1:] == '"':
src/requests/utils.py:434:            item = unquote_header_value(item[1:-1])
src/requests/utils.py:435:        result.append(item)
src/requests/utils.py:436:    return result

src/requests/utils.py:440:def parse_dict_header(value: str) -> dict[str, str | None]:
src/requests/utils.py:441:    """Parse lists of key, value pairs as described by RFC 2068 Section 2 and
src/requests/utils.py:442:    convert them into a python dict:
src/requests/utils.py:443:
src/requests/utils.py:444:    >>> d = parse_dict_header('foo="is a fish", bar="as well"')
src/requests/utils.py:445:    >>> type(d) is dict
src/requests/utils.py:446:    True
src/requests/utils.py:447:    >>> sorted(d.items())
src/requests/utils.py:448:    [('bar', 'as well'), ('foo', 'is a fish')]
src/requests/utils.py:449:
src/requests/utils.py:450:    If there is no value for a key it will be `None`:
src/requests/utils.py:451:
src/requests/utils.py:452:    >>> parse_dict_header('key_without_value')
src/requests/utils.py:453:    {'key_without_value': None}
src/requests/utils.py:454:
src/requests/utils.py:455:    To create a header from the :class:`dict` again, use the
src/requests/utils.py:456:    :func:`dump_header` function.
src/requests/utils.py:457:
src/requests/utils.py:458:    :param value: a string with a dict header.
src/requests/utils.py:459:    :return: :class:`dict`
src/requests/utils.py:460:    :rtype: dict
src/requests/utils.py:461:    """
src/requests/utils.py:462:    result: dict[str, str | None] = {}
src/requests/utils.py:463:    for item in _parse_list_header(value):
src/requests/utils.py:464:        if "=" not in item:
src/requests/utils.py:465:            result[item] = None

src/requests/utils.py:705:    """Re-quote the given URI.
src/requests/utils.py:706:
src/requests/utils.py:707:    This function passes the given URI through an unquote/quote cycle to

src/requests/utils.py:710:    :rtype: str

src/requests/utils.py:712:    safe_with_percent = "!#$%&'()*+,/:;=?@[]~"

src/requests/utils.py:714:    try:

src/requests/utils.py:1008:def guess_json_utf(data: bytes) -> str | None:
src/requests/utils.py:1009:    """
src/requests/utils.py:1010:    :rtype: str
src/requests/utils.py:1011:    """
src/requests/utils.py:1012:    # JSON always starts with two ASCII characters, so detection is as
src/requests/utils.py:1013:    # easy as counting the nulls and from their location and count
src/requests/utils.py:1014:    # determine the encoding. Also detect a BOM, if present.
src/requests/utils.py:1015:    sample = data[:4]
src/requests/utils.py:1016:    if sample in (codecs.BOM_UTF32_LE, codecs.BOM_UTF32_BE):
src/requests/utils.py:1017:        return "utf-32"  # BOM included
src/requests/utils.py:1018:    if sample[:3] == codecs.BOM_UTF8:
src/requests/utils.py:1019:        return "utf-8-sig"  # BOM included, MS style (discouraged)
src/requests/utils.py:1020:    if sample[:2] in (codecs.BOM_UTF16_LE, codecs.BOM_UTF16_BE):
src/requests/utils.py:1021:        return "utf-16"  # BOM included
src/requests/utils.py:1022:    nullcount = sample.count(_null)
src/requests/utils.py:1023:    if nullcount == 0:
src/requests/utils.py:1024:        return "utf-8"
src/requests/utils.py:1025:    if nullcount == 2:
src/requests/utils.py:1026:        if sample[::2] == _null2:  # 1st and 3rd are null
src/requests/utils.py:1027:            return "utf-16-be"
src/requests/utils.py:1028:        if sample[1::2] == _null2:  # 2nd and 4th are null
src/requests/utils.py:1029:            return "utf-16-le"
src/requests/utils.py:1030:        # Did not detect 2 valid UTF-16 ascii-range characters
src/requests/utils.py:1031:    if nullcount == 3:
src/requests/utils.py:1032:        if sample[:3] == _null3:
src/requests/utils.py:1033:            return "utf-32-be"
src/requests/utils.py:1034:        if sample[1:] == _null3:
src/requests/utils.py:1035:            return "utf-32-le"
src/requests/utils.py:1036:        # Did not detect a valid UTF-32 ascii-range character
src/requests/utils.py:1037:    return None

tests/test_lowlevel.py:127:def test_digestauth_401_count_reset_on_redirect():
tests/test_lowlevel.py:128:    """Ensure we correctly reset num_401_calls after a successful digest auth,
tests/test_lowlevel.py:129:    followed by a 302 redirect to another digest auth prompt.
tests/test_lowlevel.py:130:
tests/test_lowlevel.py:131:    See https://github.com/psf/requests/issues/1979.
tests/test_lowlevel.py:132:    """
tests/test_lowlevel.py:133:    text_401 = (b'HTTP/1.1 401 UNAUTHORIZED\r\n'
tests/test_lowlevel.py:134:                b'Content-Length: 0\r\n'
tests/test_lowlevel.py:135:                b'WWW-Authenticate: Digest nonce="6bf5d6e4da1ce66918800195d6b9130d"'
tests/test_lowlevel.py:136:                b', opaque="372825293d1c26955496c80ed6426e9e", '
tests/test_lowlevel.py:137:                b'realm="me@kennethreitz.com", qop=auth\r\n\r\n')
tests/test_lowlevel.py:138:
tests/test_lowlevel.py:139:    text_302 = (b'HTTP/1.1 302 FOUND\r\n'
tests/test_lowlevel.py:140:                b'Content-Length: 0\r\n'
tests/test_lowlevel.py:141:                b'Location: /\r\n\r\n')
tests/test_lowlevel.py:142:
tests/test_lowlevel.py:143:    text_200 = (b'HTTP/1.1 200 OK\r\n'
tests/test_lowlevel.py:144:                b'Content-Length: 0\r\n\r\n')
tests/test_lowlevel.py:145:
tests/test_lowlevel.py:146:    expected_digest = (b'Authorization: Digest username="user", '
tests/test_lowlevel.py:147:                       b'realm="me@kennethreitz.com", '
tests/test_lowlevel.py:148:                       b'nonce="6bf5d6e4da1ce66918800195d6b9130d", uri="/"')
tests/test_lowlevel.py:149:
tests/test_lowlevel.py:150:    auth = requests.auth.HTTPDigestAuth('user', 'pass')
tests/test_lowlevel.py:151:
tests/test_lowlevel.py:152:    def digest_response_handler(sock):
tests/test_lowlevel.py:153:        # Respond to initial GET with a challenge.
tests/test_lowlevel.py:154:        request_content = consume_socket_content(sock, timeout=0.5)
tests/test_lowlevel.py:155:        assert request_content.startswith(b"GET / HTTP/1.1")
tests/test_lowlevel.py:156:        sock.send(text_401)
tests/test_lowlevel.py:157:
tests/test_lowlevel.py:158:        # Verify we receive an Authorization header in response, then redirect.
tests/test_lowlevel.py:159:        request_content = consume_socket_content(sock, timeout=0.5)
tests/test_lowlevel.py:160:        assert expected_digest in request_content
tests/test_lowlevel.py:161:        sock.send(text_302)
tests/test_lowlevel.py:162:
tests/test_lowlevel.py:163:        # Verify Authorization isn't sent to the redirected host,
tests/test_lowlevel.py:164:        # then send another challenge.
tests/test_lowlevel.py:165:        request_content = consume_socket_content(sock, timeout=0.5)
tests/test_lowlevel.py:166:        assert b'Authorization:' not in request_content
tests/test_lowlevel.py:167:        sock.send(text_401)
tests/test_lowlevel.py:168:
tests/test_lowlevel.py:169:        # Verify Authorization is sent correctly again, and return 200 OK.
tests/test_lowlevel.py:170:        request_content = consume_socket_content(sock, timeout=0.5)
tests/test_lowlevel.py:171:        assert expected_digest in request_content
tests/test_lowlevel.py:172:        sock.send(text_200)
tests/test_lowlevel.py:173:
tests/test_lowlevel.py:174:        return request_content
tests/test_lowlevel.py:175:
tests/test_lowlevel.py:176:    close_server = threading.Event()
tests/test_lowlevel.py:177:    server = Server(digest_response_handler, wait_to_close_event=close_server)
tests/test_lowlevel.py:178:
tests/test_lowlevel.py:179:    with server as (host, port):
tests/test_lowlevel.py:180:        url = f'http://{host}:{port}/'

tests/test_lowlevel.py:182:        # Verify server succeeded in authenticating.
tests/test_lowlevel.py:183:        assert r.status_code == 200
tests/test_lowlevel.py:184:        # Verify Authorization was sent in final request.
tests/test_lowlevel.py:185:        assert 'Authorization' in r.request.headers

tests/test_requests.py:2287:        """Tests a custom mixin to overwrite ``get_redirect_target``.
tests/test_requests.py:2288:
tests/test_requests.py:2289:        Ensures a subclassed ``requests.Session`` can handle a certain type of
tests/test_requests.py:2290:        malformed redirect responses.
tests/test_requests.py:2291:
tests/test_requests.py:2292:        1. original request receives a proper response: 302 redirect
tests/test_requests.py:2293:        2. following the redirect, a malformed response is given:
tests/test_requests.py:2294:            status code = HTTP 200
tests/test_requests.py:2295:            location = alternate url
tests/test_requests.py:2296:        3. the custom session catches the edge case and follows the redirect
tests/test_requests.py:2297:        """
tests/test_requests.py:2298:        url_final = httpbin("html")
tests/test_requests.py:2299:        querystring_malformed = urlencode({"location": url_final})

tests/test_requests.py:2301:        querystring_redirect = urlencode({"url": url_redirect_malformed})
tests/test_requests.py:2302:        url_redirect = httpbin("redirect-to?%s" % querystring_redirect)
tests/test_requests.py:2303:        urls_test = [
tests/test_requests.py:2304:            url_redirect,
tests/test_requests.py:2305:            url_redirect_malformed,
tests/test_requests.py:2306:            url_final,
tests/test_requests.py:2307:        ]
tests/test_requests.py:2308:
tests/test_requests.py:2309:        class CustomRedirectSession(requests.Session):
tests/test_requests.py:2310:            def get_redirect_target(self, resp):
tests/test_requests.py:2311:                # default behavior
tests/test_requests.py:2312:                if resp.is_redirect:
tests/test_requests.py:2313:                    return resp.headers["location"]
tests/test_requests.py:2314:                # edge case - check to see if 'location' is in headers anyways
tests/test_requests.py:2315:                location = resp.headers.get("location")
tests/test_requests.py:2316:                if location and (location != resp.url):
tests/test_requests.py:2317:                    return location
tests/test_requests.py:2318:                return None
tests/test_requests.py:2319:
tests/test_requests.py:2320:        session = CustomRedirectSession()
